<user>
What is the CSULB policy on freedom of speech?
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 96-18 (Computing Resources, Access to and Use of CSULB), section 2.1.2 (Freedom of Speech): CSULB respects the principle of academic freedom and does not attempt to censor authorized user's electronic messages or publications. If there is any doubt, users must include caveats to make it clear that they speak only for themselves, and not the University. Threats to or harassment of other users or groups whether on or off campus does not fall within the bounds of this protection and will not be tolerated.
Source: https://web.csulb.edu/divisions/academic_affairs/grad_undergrad/senate/policy/academic/alphabetical/documents/96-18PolicyGoverningAccesstoandUseofCSULBComputingResources.pdf
</bot>

<user>
Can you explain the "Notice of Allegations" part of the Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Jeanne Clery Campus Safety Act, section "Notice of Allegations": Where Parties assert Formal Complaints against each other, and they arise out of the same events or circumstances, the Title IX Coordinator may consolidate the Formal Complaints into one. When the Title IX Coordinator receives a Formal Complaint, the Title IX Coordinator will Simultaneously provide both Parties a written notice of allegations.

I have a question about accident investigation at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>

Policy on Class Scheduling - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>

Harassing conduct, whether in the form of verbal, physical, or verbal, is strictly prohibited.

Capitalization - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Business Property, section 1.0 (Capitalization): 1.0 ASI property shall be capitalized for accounting purposes when it represents an investment of $10,000 or more per unit and has an estimate useful life of at least three years. Capitalization means the asset is recorded in the accounting records as a fixed asset and accountability is maintained until disposal of the asset occurs.

I have a question about hazing at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Regulation VIII: Recognition And Registration For Student Organizations, section "Hazing": Any method or act of initiation or preinitiation into (or the maintenance of status within) a student organization or student body, whether or not the organization or body is officially recognized by the CSU, which is likely to cause serious bodily injury or mental harm to any former, current, or prospective student of the CSU is prohibited and constitutes a violation of this policy.
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/student-affairs/campus-rules-and-regulations/regulation-viii

</user>
<bot>

Although the final authority to make either of these decisions rests with the President of the University and his or her designees, traditionally faculty are more involved in the making of educational policy while administrators make resource allocation decisions.

The associated payee information shall be entered into the unclaimed property database so that the check does not escheat to the state.

<user>
Use of Other Generated Revenue - what are the rules at CSULB?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Agency Accounts, section "Use of Other Generated Revenue": All other revenue for which restrictions have not been indicated shall be treated by the Business Office as agency funds. By this definition, such funds shall not fall under the budgetary and programmatic control of the ASI and may be used for purposes otherwise prohibited by ASI policy, provided they are not otherwise prohibited by university or system-wide policy.
Source: https://asicsulb.org/corporate/documents/discover/policies/Policy%20on%20Agency%20Accounts%20103025.pdf
</bot>

What does the CSULB Policies and Protocols say about on and off campus political events?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policies and Protocols, section "On and Off Campus Political Events": Events that are political in nature - hosted by a political party or candidate for office - that do not have a legislative purpose, may not be hosted, co-sponsored, or sponsored by the campus, and must cover the full costs of the event activity on campus. No Public Funds may be expended to support a political event on campus as that would be considered a gift of public funds.

What does the CSULB Policy on Student Media say about material that unlawfully invades a person's right to privacy?
</user>
<bot>
According to the Policy on Student Media, section 2.3 (Material that Unlawfully Invades a Person's Right to Privacy): 2.4 Material that Will Cause "A Material and Substantial Disruption of Campus Activities" Disruption includes student rioting, unlawful seizures of property, destruction of property, or substantial student participation in a campus boycott, sit-in, walk-out, or other related form of activity.

<user>
I have a question about the program of study at CSULB. What does the policy say?
</user>
<bot>
According to CSULB Policy Statement 25-11 (Policy on Master's Degrees), section 6.0 (The Program of Study): 6.1 If a program of study as outlined in the University Catalog allows for multiple course options, then the student must develop a program of study in collaboration with their graduate advisor or designee. 6.2 Once a student is advanced to candidacy with an approved program of study, the program of study may not be changed without the consent of the academic unit and graduate student, except for changing the type of culminating activity.
Source: https://www.csulb.edu/academic-senate/policy-masters-degrees
